# Proposed OEIS comments: A279960 to A283348

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A279960

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A279960>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [4038](../papers/04001-04500/04038-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Dec 24 2016: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 9 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

## A279971

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A279971>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [1052](../papers/01001-01500/01052-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 9*a(n-1) - 30*a(n-2) + 45*a(n-3) - 30*a(n-4) + 9*a(n-5) -a(n-6).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(1 - 2*x)*(1 - 4*x + 4*x^2 + 3*x^3) / (1 - 3*x + x^2)^3. - _Colin Barker_, Feb 12 2019

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 51 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 7. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A279977

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A279977>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [3023](../papers/03001-03500/03023-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical for column k:
> Empirical for row n:

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical column recurrences are true. Each column counts arrays of a fixed width, and counting those a row at a time gives a transfer matrix, so every column satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most the number of states; checking the ones above is then a finite exact computation, and they hold. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A280367

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A280367>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [4073](../papers/04001-04500/04073-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Jan 01 2017: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 8 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A280410

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A280410>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [4134](../papers/04001-04500/04134-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Jan 03 2017: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 6 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A280553>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [806](../papers/00501-01000/00806-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 6*a(n-1) - 12*a(n-2) + 5*a(n-3) + 12*a(n-4) - 12*a(n-5) - 3*a(n-6) + 6*a(n-7) - a(n-9) for n>12.
> Empirical g.f.: x*(13 + 322*x - 928*x^2 + 21*x^3 + 930*x^4 + 341*x^5 - 79*x^6 - 480*x^7 - 148*x^8 - 205*x^9 - 238*x^10 + 6*x^11) / ((1 - x)^3*(1 - x - x^2)^3). - _Colin Barker_, Feb 13 2019

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 49 155 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 12. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A281146

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A281146>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [4077](../papers/04001-04500/04077-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Feb 22 2017: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 8 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A281214

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A281214>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [4002](../papers/04001-04500/04002-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Jan 18 2017: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 12 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A281473

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A281473>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [2848](../papers/02501-03000/02848-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) - a(n-2) + a(n-3) - a(n-4) + a(n-5) -a (n-6) for n>11.
> Empirical g.f.: x*(16 + 232*x - 379*x^2 + 189*x^3 - 251*x^4 + 124*x^5 - 288*x^6 + 132*x^7 - 24*x^8 + x^9 + 2*x^10) / ((1 - x)*(1 - x - x^3 - x^5)). - _Colin Barker_, Feb 19 2019

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 561 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A282002

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A282002>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [4106](../papers/04001-04500/04106-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Feb 05 2017: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 7 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A282142

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A282142>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [4112](../papers/04001-04500/04112-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Feb 27 2017: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 7 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 8 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A282438

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A282438>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [10691](../papers/10501-11000/10691-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 88 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 217 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 88, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A282577

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A282577>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [4113](../papers/04001-04500/04113-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Feb 27 2017: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 7 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 125 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 60, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A282796

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A282796>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [4142](../papers/04001-04500/04142-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Feb 21 2017: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 6 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [10708](../papers/10501-11000/10708-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 86 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 125 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 86, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

## A282911

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A282911>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [3996](../papers/03501-04000/03996-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Feb 25 2017: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 13 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

## A283200

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A283200>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [10616](../papers/10501-11000/10616-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 96 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 121 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 96, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

## A283214

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A283214>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [4005](../papers/04001-04500/04005-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Mar 04 2017: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 12 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 394 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 38. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A283348

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A283348>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [4029](../papers/04001-04500/04029-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Mar 06 2017: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The conjectured generating function is correct. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix on 10 states, and the generating function of a walk count is 1/det(I - xM) times a polynomial, so it is a ratio of two polynomials whose degrees are at most the number of states. The conjectured expression is another such ratio, of known degree, and two of them are equal as soon as enough coefficients agree: cross-multiplying leaves a polynomial whose degree is bounded, so it vanishes once that many coefficients match. Enough coefficients were compared in exact arithmetic. It follows that a(n) satisfies the linear recurrence whose coefficients are read off the denominator, which this entry does not currently record. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```
